F.4
Interpreting the Latent Groups

The classification is fully data–driven, but it is not completely a black box.

What drives the classification.

As seen from Figure
1
, the blue dots and red dots are well separated by a horizontal line in Panel (a) and by a vertical line in Panel (e), but are evenly mixed otherwise. Consequently, the classification is mostly driven by coefficient
\hat{\pi}_{i1}
before
B_{1}(\tau_{t})
(time trend term in the intercept) and the coefficient
\hat{\pi}_{i8}
before
y_{it2}
(level term before non-consumer loans).

What differs technologically.

Figures
2
display the group–specific, time–varying
coefficients
\{\widehat{\beta}_{k,\ell}(\tau)\}
and intercepts
\widehat{\alpha}_{k}(\tau)
. Two facts are visible:
(i) the levels/curvature of input elasticities differ across groups, and (ii) their time profiles
(drifts) are not parallel. This is exactly the dimension our HAC step is designed to capture:
firms in the same group share the same coefficient paths; firms in different groups load inputs
and time differently (technology regimes).

We summarize certain statistics for each group below. These numbers indicate correlations only and do not suggest any causal relationship. We find that banks in group 1 are, on average, larger, more efficient (closer to their frontier), and exhibit less variation in inefficiency than banks in group 2.

What differs in performance.

To separate technology from performance,
we summarize the mean and standard deviation of
\widehat{\textrm{E}}\left(\alpha_{i}^{0}+u_{i}|\varepsilon_{i1},...,\varepsilon_{iT}\right)
using (
C.4
), within each group.
Group 1 has the mean (standard deviation) at
0.0923

(2.17\times 10^{-4})
with 113 observations, while Group 2 has its mean (standard deviation) at
0.1130

(0.0558)
with 353 observations. The group means are statistically different at 5% level of significance using

\frac{\hat{\mu}_{1}-\hat{\mu}_{2}}{\sqrt{s_{1}^{2}/n_{1}+s_{2}^{2}/n_{2}}}=-6.6179.

From the mean values, banks in Group 2 appear less efficient than those in Group 1. The standard deviations also indicate that inefficiency varies more within Group 2 than within Group 1.

Who is more often in each group.

We compare bank sizes across the two groups and find the following. For group 1, the mean (standard deviation) of
\log(\text{size})
is
13.4866

(1.8689)
based on 113 observations. For group 2, the corresponding mean (standard deviation) is
12.4813

(1.5012)
based on 353 observations. The difference in group means is statistically significant at the 5% level, with a t-statistic of
6.9721
. Thus, on average, banks in group 1 are larger.

F.5
Additional Figure for the Empirical Application

Figure F.4:
Inefficiency Estimates of 60 Banks in Descending Order

•

Note
: Left panel shows the inefficiency estimates in the homogeneous case where all banks are treated as one group with uniquely distributed error term, while the right panel shows the inefficiency estimates in the heterogeneous case where banks are classified into groups and inefficiency estimates to possess a mixture distribution structure. The numbers to the right of each bar corresponds to their respective bank ID, “IDRSSD” from
https://cdr.ffiec.gov/public/PWS/DownloadBulkData.aspx
.

Appendix G
On
\mathbb{I}
in Theorem
3.2

Without loss of generality, we show the properties of
\mathbb{I}

in the case when
\mathcal{K}^{*}=2
to simplify notation. The cases
when
\mathcal{K}^{*}>2
can be similarly shown.

G.1
First and Second Order Derivatives

We use the notation in Appendix
C
. We assume
\alpha_{i}
,

\beta_{i}
, and
\sigma_{vi}^{2}
are known because they are not
the parameters of interest in this appendix.

We first discuss the case when the distribution does not have a latent
structure. The unknown parameters are
\left(\alpha^{0},\sigma_{u}^{2}\right)

in this case. Note

\varepsilon_{it}=y_{it}-\alpha^{0}-\alpha\left(\tau_{t}\right)-x_{it}^{\prime}\beta\left(\tau_{t}\right)=v_{it}-u_{i},

\displaystyle f\left(\left.y_{i}\right|x_{i};\alpha^{0},\sigma_{u}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\sigma_{vi}^{T-1}\sqrt{\sigma_{vi}^{2}+T\sigma_{u}^{2}}}\left[1-\Phi\left(\frac{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{it}}{\sigma_{vi}\sqrt{\sigma_{vi}^{2}/\sigma_{u}^{2}+T}}\right)\right]\left[\frac{1}{\left(2\pi\right)^{T/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{it}^{2}}{2\sigma_{vi}^{2}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\times\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\frac{\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{it}\right)^{2}}{\sigma_{vi}^{2}\left(\sigma_{vi}^{2}/\sigma_{u}^{2}+T\right)}\right),

and

\displaystyle\log f\left(\left.y_{i}\right|x_{i};\alpha^{0},\sigma_{u}^{2}\right)=

\displaystyle C-\frac{(T-1)}{2}\log\sigma_{vi}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\text{$\log$}\left(\sigma_{vi}^{2}+T\sigma_{u}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\log\left[1-\Phi\left(-\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}\right)\right]+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}\right)^{2}-\frac{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{it}^{2}}{2\sigma_{vi}^{2}},

with
\sigma_{i}^{2}=\sigma_{vi}^{2}+T\sigma_{u}^{2},

\rho_{i}=\sigma_{u}/\sigma_{vi},

\mu_{i\ast}=-\sigma_{u}^{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{it}/\sigma_{i}^{2},

\sigma_{i\ast}^{2}=\sigma_{u}^{2}\sigma_{vi}^{2}/\sigma_{i}^{2}
,
and a
C
that does not depend on parameters.

By some straightforward calculations,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma_{u}^{2}}\log f\left(\left.y_{i}\right|x_{i};\alpha^{0},\sigma_{u}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{T}{\sigma_{vi}^{2}+T\sigma_{u}^{2}}+\frac{\phi\left(-\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}\right)}{1-\Phi\left(-\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}\right)}\frac{-\sigma_{vi}}{2\sigma_{u}^{4}\left(\sigma_{vi}^{2}/\sigma_{u}^{2}+T\right)^{3/2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{it}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\sigma_{u}^{4}\left(\sigma_{vi}^{2}/\sigma_{u}^{2}+T\right)^{2}}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{it}\right)^{2},

(G.1)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\alpha^{0}}\log f\left(\left.y_{i}\right|x_{i};\alpha^{0},\sigma_{u}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\phi\left(-\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}\right)}{1-\Phi\left(-\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}\right)}\frac{T}{\sigma_{vi}\sqrt{\sigma_{vi}^{2}/\sigma_{u}^{2}+T}}+\frac{1}{T^{-1}\sigma_{vi}^{2}+\sigma_{u}^{2}}\frac{1}{T}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{it}\right),

(G.2)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma_{u}^{2}\partial\alpha^{0}}\log f\left(\left.y_{i}\right|x_{i};\alpha^{0},\sigma_{u}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=g\left(\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}\right)\frac{-T}{2\sigma_{u}^{4}\left(\sigma_{vi}^{2}/\sigma_{u}^{2}+T\right)^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{it}+\frac{\phi\left(-\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}\right)}{1-\Phi\left(-\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}\right)}\frac{T\sigma_{vi}}{2\sigma_{u}^{4}\left(\sigma_{vi}^{2}/\sigma_{u}^{2}+T\right)^{3/2}}

\displaystyle-\frac{T}{\sigma_{u}^{4}\left(\sigma_{vi}^{2}/\sigma_{u}^{2}+T\right)^{2}}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{it}\right),

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial(\alpha^{0})^{2}}\log f\left(\left.y_{i}\right|x_{i};\alpha^{0},\sigma_{u}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=g\left(\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}\right)\frac{T^{2}}{\sigma_{vi}^{2}\left(\sigma_{vi}^{2}/\sigma_{u}^{2}+T\right)}-\frac{1}{T^{-1}\sigma_{vi}^{2}+\sigma_{u}^{2}},

and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\left(\sigma_{u}^{2}\right)^{2}}\log f\left(\left.y_{i}\right|x_{i};\alpha^{0},\sigma_{u}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\frac{T^{2}}{\left(\sigma_{vi}^{2}+T\sigma_{u}^{2}\right)^{2}}+g\left(\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}\right)\frac{\sigma_{vi}^{2}}{4\sigma_{u}^{8}\left(\sigma_{vi}^{2}/\sigma_{u}^{2}+T\right)^{3}}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{it}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\phi\left(-\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}\right)}{1-\Phi\left(-\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}\right)}\frac{\left(\sigma_{vi}^{3}\sigma_{u}^{-4/3}+4T\sigma_{vi}\sigma_{u}^{2/3}\right)}{4\left(\sigma_{vi}^{2}\sigma_{u}^{2/3}+T\sigma_{u}^{8/3}\right)^{5/2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{it}-\frac{T}{\left(\sigma_{vi}^{2}+T\sigma_{u}^{2}\right)^{3}}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{it}\right)^{2},

where

g\left(\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}\right)=\left.\frac{d}{ds}\frac{\phi\left(-s\right)}{1-\Phi\left(-s\right)}\right|_{s=\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}}=\left.\frac{-\phi\left(-s\right)-\phi^{\prime}\left(-s\right)\left[1-\phi\left(-s\right)\right]}{\left[1-\Phi\left(-s\right)\right]^{2}}\right|_{s=\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}}.

In the case when the distribution possesses a latent structure, the
log likelihood function becomes

\log\left[\tau f\left(y_{i}\left|x_{i};\alpha_{(1)}^{0},\sigma_{u(1)}^{2}\right.\right)+\left(1-\tau\right)f\left(\left.y_{i}\right|x_{i};\alpha_{(2)}^{0},\sigma_{u(2)}^{2}\right)\right]\equiv\log\left[\tau f_{1i}+\left(1-\tau\right)f_{2i}\right].

Then,

\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\log\left[\tau f_{1i}+\left(1-\tau\right)f_{2i}\right]=\frac{f_{1i}-f_{2i}}{\tau f_{1i}+\left(1-\tau\right)f_{2i}},\text{ and}

\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\tau^{2}}\log\left[\tau f_{1i}+\left(1-\tau\right)f_{2i}\right]=-\frac{\left(f_{1i}-f_{2i}\right)^{2}}{\left[\tau f_{1i}+\left(1-\tau\right)f_{2i}\right]^{2}}.

The derivatives with respect to other arguments
\alpha^{0}
and

\sigma_{u}^{2}
can be derived using the chain rule. For example,

\frac{\partial}{\partial\alpha_{(1)}^{0}}\log\left[\tau f_{1i}+\left(1-\tau\right)f_{2i}\right]=\frac{\tau\frac{\partial}{\partial\alpha_{(1)}^{0}}f_{1i}}{\tau f_{1i}+\left(1-\tau\right)f_{2i}}=\frac{\tau f_{1i}}{\tau f_{1i}+\left(1-\tau\right)f_{2i}}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial\alpha_{(1)}^{0}}\log f_{1i},\text{ and}

\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma_{u(1)}^{2}}\log\left[\tau f_{1i}+\left(1-\tau\right)f_{2i}\right]=\frac{\tau\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma_{u(1)}^{2}}f_{1i}}{\tau f_{1i}+\left(1-\tau\right)f_{2i}}=\frac{\tau f_{1i}}{\tau f_{1i}+\left(1-\tau\right)f_{2i}}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma_{u(1)}^{2}}\log f_{1i}.

G.2
Properties of
\mathbb{I}

We verify that
\mathbb{I}
behaves like a regular positive definite
matrix in this section. The intuition of this result is as follows.
Note
\tilde{f}_{i}\left(\varrho_{0}\right)
is the density function
for
\varepsilon_{it}=v_{it}-u_{i}
, and the parameters of interest
are on the distribution of
u_{i}
only. In other words,
v_{it}

are nuisance for the estimation. Mathematically, one can see that
the dominant components in
\frac{\partial}{\partial\varrho}\log\tilde{f}_{i}\left(\varrho\right)

are functions of
u_{i}
and do not grow as
T\rightarrow\infty
.
Thus, the convergence rate of
\hat{\varrho}
is
\sqrt{N}
based
on this intuition.

Without loss of generality, we assume that there is no group structure
for the frontiers and
\sigma_{vi}^{2}
, that is,
K^{*}=1
. We
show the result by assuming
\alpha^{0}=\alpha_{(1)}^{0}=\alpha_{(2)}^{0},

and
\sigma_{u(1)}^{2}\neq\sigma_{u(2)}^{2}
. The case with
\alpha_{(1)}^{0}\neq\alpha_{(2)}^{0}

can be similarly handled but with more tedious discussions. We show
the result by using the following identity

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}

\displaystyle=\left.-\text{E}\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\varrho\partial\varrho^{\prime}}\log\tilde{f}_{i}\left(\varrho\right)\right]\right|_{\varrho=\varrho^{0}}

\displaystyle=\left.\text{E}\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial\varrho}\log\tilde{f}_{i}\left(\varrho\right)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial\varrho^{\prime}}\log\tilde{f}_{i}\left(\varrho\right)\right]\right|_{\varrho=\varrho^{0}}.

(G.3)

From the forms of
\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma_{uj}^{2}}\log\left[\tau f_{1}+\left(1-\tau\right)f_{2}\right],

\frac{\partial}{\partial\alpha_{j}^{0}}\log\left[\tau f_{1}+\left(1-\tau\right)f_{2}\right],

j=1,2
, and

\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\log\left[\tau f_{1}+\left(1-\tau\right)f_{2}\right]

in Appendix
G.1
, they are clearly not linear
dependent. If
T
is fixed,
\mathbb{I}
is just the information
matrix for a regular likelihood function, and it is positive definite.
Our analysis is complicated by the fact that
T\rightarrow\infty
.
The diagonal of
\mathbb{I}
might explode as
T\rightarrow\infty
.
Thus, it suffices to show that the diagonals of
\mathbb{I}
behave
like regular positive constants, and we show that in the below.

The following is useful for the analysis. At the true values of parameters,

\varepsilon_{it}=v_{it}-u_{i}.
So

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{it}

\displaystyle=-u_{i}+T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}v_{it}

\displaystyle=-u_{i}+O_{P}\left(T^{-1/2}\right).

Since
-u_{i}
is negative,
T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{it}

is negative with very high probability. Another implication is
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{it}\propto_{P}-T+O_{P}\left(T^{1/2}\right).

Using the definition of
\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}},

\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}=\frac{-1}{\sigma_{vi}\sqrt{\sigma_{vi}^{2}/\sigma_{u}^{2}+T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{it}\propto_{P}\sqrt{T}+O_{P}\left(1\right).

The above implies
\Phi\left(-\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}\right)=o_{P}\left(1\right),

\frac{\phi\left(-\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}\right)}{1-\Phi\left(-\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}\right)}\propto_{P}\exp\left(-T\right),\quad\frac{\phi\left(-\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}\right)}{1-\Phi\left(-\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}\right)}\sqrt{T}=o_{P}\left(1\right),

and

g\left(\frac{\mu_{i\ast}}{\sigma_{i\ast}}\right)\propto_{P}\exp\left(-T\right).

Applying the above for (
G.2
),

\frac{\partial}{\partial\alpha^{0}}\log f\left(\left.y_{i}\right|x_{i};\alpha^{0},\sigma_{u}^{2}\right)=-u_{i}+E\left(u_{i}\right)+o_{P}\left(1\right).

Similarly (
G.1
) implies

\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma_{u}^{2}}\log f\left(\left.y_{i}\right|x_{i};\alpha^{0},\sigma_{u}^{2}\right)=\frac{u_{i}^{2}}{2\sigma_{u}^{4}}-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{u}^{2}}+o_{P}\left(1\right).

Some tedious yet straightforward calculations can yield

f\left(y_{i}\left|x_{i};\alpha^{0},\sigma_{u(1)}^{2}\right.\right)\propto_{P}f\left(\left.y_{i}\right|x_{i};\alpha^{0},\sigma_{u(2)}^{2}\right),

and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\log\left[\tau f_{1i}+\left(1-\tau\right)f_{2i}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{f_{1i}-f_{2i}}{\tau f_{1i}+\left(1-\tau\right)f_{2i}}.

\displaystyle=\frac{1-\sqrt{\left.\sigma_{u(1)}^{2}\right/\sigma_{u(2)}^{2}}\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{\sigma_{u(1)}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{u(2)}^{2}}\right)u_{i}^{2}\right)}{\tau+\left(1-\tau\right)\sqrt{\left.\sigma_{u(1)}^{2}\right/\sigma_{2u}^{2}}\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{\sigma_{u(1)}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{u(2)}^{2}}\right)u_{i}^{2}\right)}\left(1+o_{P}\left(1\right)\right).

The variance of the leading terms in
\frac{\partial}{\partial\alpha^{0}}\log f
,

\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma_{u}^{2}}\log f,
and
\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\log\left[\tau f_{1i}+\left(1-\tau\right)f_{2i}\right]

are functions of
u_{i}
, and they are bounded and bounded away from
zero.

Finally, since
f_{1i}\propto_{P}f_{2i},

\frac{\partial}{\partial\alpha_{j}^{0}}\log\left[\tau f_{1i}+\left(1-\tau\right)f_{2i}\right],

j=1,2,
enjoys similar properties as
\frac{\partial}{\partial\alpha^{0}}\log f
.
This is also the case for
\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma_{uj}^{2}}\log\left[\tau f_{1i}+\left(1-\tau\right)f_{2i}\right],
j=1,2.

From the form of the derivatives, clearly,
\frac{\partial}{\partial\varrho_{j}}\log\left[\tau f_{1i}+\left(1-\tau\right)f_{2i}\right]
,

j=1,2,...,5,
are not linearly dependent. Together with the fact
that the variance of them are bounded and bounded away from zero,
then for any
5\times 1
vector
a,

a^{\prime}\left.\text{E}\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial\varrho}\log\tilde{f}_{i}\left(\varrho\right)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial\varrho^{\prime}}\log\tilde{f}_{i}\left(\varrho\right)\right]\right|_{\varrho=\varrho^{0}}a\propto\left\|a\right\|^{2}.

This implies that
\mathbb{I}
must be positive definite with bounded
eigenvalues, using the identity in (
G.3
).

Appendix H
Technical Lemmas

We collect technical lemmas and their proofs in this section. For
an easier reference, we present the bodies of those lemmas first,
followed by proofs. We note the lemmas of probability bounds below
are related to those developed in
Atak et al. (2025)
.

Remark H.1

(Some inequalities)
.

We may apply
the following inequalities in the proofs directly without referring
them back to here. First

\Pr\left(X_{1}+X_{2}\geq C\right)\leq\Pr\left(X_{1}\geq\pi C\right)+\Pr\left(X_{2}\geq\left(1-\pi\right)C\right)

for any constant
\pi.
That is because
\left\{X_{1}+X_{2}\geq C\right\}\subseteq\left\{X_{1}\geq\pi C\right\}\cup\left\{X_{1}\geq\left(1-\pi\right)C\right\}.

Similarly, we have

\Pr\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\geq C\right)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Pr\left(X_{i}\geq Cn^{-1}\right)\ \text{\emph{and} }\Pr\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq n}X_{i}\geq C\right)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Pr\left(X_{i}\geq C\right).

For any positive random variables
X_{1}
and
X_{2}

and positive constants
C_{1}
and
C_{2},

\Pr\left(X_{1}\cdot X_{2}\geq C_{1}\right)\leq\Pr\left(X_{1}\geq C_{1}/C_{2}\right)+\Pr\left(X_{2}\geq C_{2}\right),

due to the fact that
\left\{X_{1}\cdot X_{2}\geq C_{1}\right\}\subseteq\left\{X_{1}\geq C_{1}/C_{2}\right\}\cup\left\{X_{2}\geq C_{2}\right\}.

Lemma H.1
.

Suppose
e_{it}
satisfies the mixing
condition across
t
in Assumption
1
(ii) and is identically
distributed across
i
. In addition
\max_{t}\text{\emph{E}}\left(\left|e_{it}\right|^{C_{q}}\right)<\infty,C_{q}>2,

T=N^{C}
for some
C>0
,
m\rightarrow\infty
as
N\rightarrow\infty
,
then

(i) for any
\epsilon>0
and any positive
1\leq\upsilon_{NT}\ll T^{1/2}\left(\log N\right)^{-2}

\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,...,N}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[e_{it}-\text{\emph{E}}\left(e_{it}\right)\right]\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{\upsilon_{NT}}\right)\lesssim\frac{NT\upsilon_{NT}^{C_{q}}\left(\log N\right)^{4C_{q}}}{T^{C_{q}}};

(ii) there exists a positive
M,
such that

\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,...,N}\max_{l=0,1,...,p}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}e_{it}b_{il}\left(\tau_{t}\right)\right|>Mm^{-\kappa}\right)\lesssim\frac{NT\left(\log N\right)^{4C_{q}}}{T^{C_{q}}},

where
b_{il}\left(\tau_{t}\right)
is defined in (
E.1
).

Lemma H.2
.

Suppose that Assumption
3

holds and
m/T\rightarrow 0
. For any
\tilde{\pi}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\left(p+1\right)},

\frac{\underline{C}_{xx}}{2}\left\|\tilde{\pi}\right\|\leq\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\text{\emph{E}}\left(\tilde{\pi}^{\prime}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\tilde{\pi}\right)\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\text{\emph{E}}\left(\pi^{\prime}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\pi\right)\leq 2\bar{C}_{xx}\left\|\tilde{\pi}\right\|,

after some large
T
.

Lemma H.3
.

Suppose that Assumptions

1
,
2
,
3
and
6

(i) hold. Then

(i) for any
\epsilon>0,

\Pr\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\sup_{\tilde{\pi}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\left(p+1\right)},\left\|\tilde{\pi}\right\|=1}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\pi}^{\prime}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\tilde{\pi}-\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\text{\emph{E}}\left(\tilde{\pi}^{\prime}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\tilde{\pi}\right)\right|>\epsilon\right)\apprle\frac{Nm^{q/2+2}\left(\log N\right)^{2q}}{T^{q/2-1}};

(ii) there exist some positive and finite
\underline{C}_{zz}
and

\bar{C}_{zz}
such that

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\underline{C}_{zz}\leq\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\mu_{\min}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\right)\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\mu_{\max}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\right)\leq\bar{C}_{zz}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-o\left(\frac{Nm^{q/2+2}\left(\log N\right)^{2q}}{T^{q/2-1}}\right)\text{.}

.

Suppose Assumptions

-
10
hold.
\tilde{\lambda}_{NT}
satisfies
\tilde{\lambda}_{NT}\rightarrow\infty

and
\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{NT}}{N}\rightarrow 0
.

\Pr\left(\hat{\mathcal{K}}\left(\tilde{\lambda}_{NT}\right)=\mathcal{K}^{*}\right)\rightarrow 1.

Proof of Lemma
H.1
.

(i)
Set

C_{NT}=\upsilon_{NT}^{-1}T\left(\log N\right)^{-4}.

Define

\mathbf{1}_{it}=\mathbf{1}\left(\left|e_{it}\right|<C_{NT}\right),\qquad\mathbf{\bar{1}}_{it}=1-\mathbf{1}_{it}.

With this notation, further define

e_{it}^{\left(1\right)}=e_{it}\mathbf{1}_{it}-\text{\emph{E}}\left(e_{it}\mathbf{1}_{it}\right),

e_{it}^{\left(2\right)}=e_{it}\mathbf{\bar{1}}_{it},\qquad e_{it}^{\left(3\right)}=-\text{\emph{E}}\left(e_{it}\mathbf{\bar{1}}_{it}\right).

Then

e_{it}-\text{\emph{E}}\left(e_{it}\right)=e_{it}^{\left(1\right)}+e_{it}^{\left(2\right)}+e_{it}^{\left(3\right)}.

By the mixing condition in Assumption
1
(ii), the moment
condition with
C_{q}>2
, and the covariance inequality for strong
mixing processes, there exists a positive finite constant
v_{0}

such that, uniformly over
i,N
, and
T
,

\max_{t}\text{\emph{Var}}\left(e_{it}^{\left(1\right)}\right)+2\max_{t}\sum_{s=t+1}^{T}\left|\text{\emph{Cov}}\left(e_{it}^{\left(1\right)},e_{is}^{\left(1\right)}\right)\right|\leq v_{0}.

Using Bernstein inequality for strong mixing processes
(e.g., Theorem 2 in
Merlevede et al. (2009)
), there exist positive
constants
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
such that

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,\ldots,N}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}e_{it}^{\left(1\right)}\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{3\upsilon_{NT}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Pr\left(\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}e_{it}^{\left(1\right)}\right|>\frac{T\epsilon}{3\upsilon_{NT}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle N\exp\left\{-\frac{C_{1}T^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{9Tv_{0}\upsilon_{NT}^{2}+9C_{NT}^{2}\upsilon_{NT}^{2}+3T\epsilon\upsilon_{NT}C_{NT}(\log T)^{2}}\right\}.

Since

C_{NT}=\upsilon_{NT}^{-1}T(\log N)^{-4},

we have

C_{NT}^{2}\upsilon_{NT}^{2}=T^{2}(\log N)^{-8}

and

T\epsilon\upsilon_{NT}C_{NT}(\log T)^{2}=T^{2}\epsilon(\log N)^{-4}(\log T)^{2}.

Moreover,

T\upsilon_{NT}^{2}=o\left\{T^{2}(\log N)^{-4}\right\}

because

\upsilon_{NT}\ll T^{1/2}(\log N)^{-2}.

Since
T=N^{C}
, we have
\log T=C\log N
, and hence

(\log N)^{-4}(\log T)^{2}\asymp(\log N)^{-2}.

It follows that, for some positive constant
C_{2}
,

\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,\ldots,N}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}e_{it}^{\left(1\right)}\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{3\upsilon_{NT}}\right)\leq N\exp\left\{-C_{2}(\log N)^{2}\right\}=o\left(N^{-M}\right)

for any sufficiently large
M>0
.

We next consider
e_{it}^{\left(2\right)}
. Notice that, if

\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq N\\
1\leq t\leq T\end{subarray}}|e_{it}|<C_{NT},

then
e_{it}^{\left(2\right)}=0
for every
i
and
t
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,\ldots,N}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}e_{it}^{\left(2\right)}\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{3\upsilon_{NT}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq N\\
1\leq t\leq T\end{subarray}}|e_{it}|\geq C_{NT}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Pr\left(|e_{it}|\geq C_{NT}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\text{\emph{E}}\left(|e_{it}|^{C_{q}}\right)}{C_{NT}^{C_{q}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle NTC_{NT}^{-C_{q}}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\frac{NT\upsilon_{NT}^{C_{q}}(\log N)^{4C_{q}}}{T^{C_{q}}}.

Finally, consider
e_{it}^{\left(3\right)}
. By the moment condition,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}e_{it}^{\left(3\right)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\text{\emph{E}}\left(|e_{it}|\mathbf{\bar{1}}_{it}\right)

\displaystyle=\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\text{\emph{E}}\left[|e_{it}|\mathbf{1}\left(|e_{it}|\geq C_{NT}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\frac{\text{\emph{E}}\left(|e_{it}|^{C_{q}}\right)}{C_{NT}^{C_{q}-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{NT}^{-(C_{q}-1)}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\upsilon_{NT}}{T}\right)^{C_{q}-1}(\log N)^{4(C_{q}-1)}.

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\upsilon_{NT}\left(\frac{\upsilon_{NT}}{T}\right)^{C_{q}-1}(\log N)^{4(C_{q}-1)}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\frac{\upsilon_{NT}^{C_{q}}(\log N)^{4(C_{q}-1)}}{T^{C_{q}-1}}=o(1).

Indeed, writing

\upsilon_{NT}=o\left\{T^{1/2}(\log N)^{-2}\right\},

the expression on the preceding display is

o\left\{T^{1-C_{q}/2}(\log N)^{2C_{q}-4}\right\}=o(1),

because
C_{q}>2
and
T=N^{C}
. Thus, uniformly over

i=1,\ldots,N
, for all sufficiently large
T
,

\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}e_{it}^{\left(3\right)}\right|<\frac{\epsilon}{3\upsilon_{NT}}.

Consequently,

\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,\ldots,N}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}e_{it}^{\left(3\right)}\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{3\upsilon_{NT}}\right)=0

for all sufficiently large
T
.

Combining the preceding three bounds yields

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,\ldots,N}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[e_{it}-\text{\emph{E}}\left(e_{it}\right)\right]\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{\upsilon_{NT}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,\ldots,N}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}e_{it}^{\left(1\right)}\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{3\upsilon_{NT}}\right)

\displaystyle+\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,\ldots,N}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}e_{it}^{\left(2\right)}\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{3\upsilon_{NT}}\right)

\displaystyle+\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,\ldots,N}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}e_{it}^{\left(3\right)}\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{3\upsilon_{NT}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\frac{NT\upsilon_{NT}^{C_{q}}(\log N)^{4C_{q}}}{T^{C_{q}}}.

(ii)
Recall (
E.4
), we can find a positive

\bar{C_{b}}
such that

\max_{l=0,...,p}\sup_{s\in\left[0,1\right]}\left|b_{il}\left(s\right)\right|\leq\bar{C_{b}}m^{-\kappa},

because
p
is finite. As a result,

\max_{i=1,...,N}\max_{l=0,1,...,p}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}e_{it}b_{il}\left(\tau_{t}\right)\right|\leq\bar{C_{b}}m^{-\kappa}\max_{i=1,...,N}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left|e_{it}\right|.

Applying (i) by setting
\upsilon_{NT}=1
for series
\left|e_{it}\right|

implies

\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,...,N}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left|e_{it}\right|-\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\text{E}\left|e_{it}\right|\right|>\epsilon\right)\lesssim\frac{NT\left(\log N\right)^{4C_{q}}}{T^{C_{q}}}.

Since
\max_{i=1,...,N}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\text{E}\left|e_{it}\right|

is bounded and
\epsilon
can be any arbitrary small positive number,
we can set

M=2\bar{C_{b}}\cdot\max_{i=1,...,N}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\text{E}\left|e_{it}\right|,

so that the desired result holds.
∎

Proof of Lemma
H.2
.

Let
g_{l}\left(\tau_{t}\right)=\pi_{l}^{\prime}\mathbb{B}^{m}\left(\tau_{t}\right)
,
where
\pi_{l}
is
m\times 1
and denotes the corresponding elements
in
\tilde{\pi}
for
x_{it,l}\otimes\mathbb{B}^{m}\left(\tau_{t}\right)
,

l=0,1,...,p
.
6
6

6

We abuse the notation a bit by letting
x_{it,0}
denote 1.

Let
\mathbf{g}\left(\tau_{t}\right)\equiv\left(g_{0}\left(\tau_{t}\right),...,g_{p}\left(\tau_{t}\right)\right)^{\prime}
.
In other words,
g_{l}\left(\tau_{t}\right)
is the approximation
for
\beta_{l}\left(\tau_{t}\right)
when
\pi_{l}
=
\pi_{l}^{0}
.
Then
\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\text{E}\left(\tilde{\pi}^{\prime}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\tilde{\pi}\right)

can be written as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\text{E}\left(\tilde{\pi}^{\prime}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\tilde{\pi}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\text{E}\left[\mathbf{g}\left(\tau_{t}\right)^{\prime}\tilde{x}_{it}\tilde{x}_{it}^{\prime}\mathbf{g}\left(\tau_{t}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{g}\left(\tau_{t}\right)^{\prime}\text{E}\left(\tilde{x}_{it}\tilde{x}_{it}^{\prime}\right)\mathbf{g}\left(\tau_{t}\right),

By the rank condition in Assumption
3
, the above implies

\displaystyle\underline{C}_{xx}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{g}\left(\tau_{t}\right)^{\prime}\mathbf{g}\left(\tau_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\text{E}\left(\tilde{\pi}^{\prime}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\tilde{\pi}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\text{E}\left(\tilde{\pi}^{\prime}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\tilde{\pi}\right)\leq\bar{C}_{xx}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{g}\left(\tau_{t}\right)^{\prime}\mathbf{g}\left(\tau_{t}\right).

(H.1)

Lemma A.4 in
Dong and Linton (2018)
implies that,
T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{B}^{m}\left(\tau_{t}\right)\mathbb{B}^{m}\left(\tau_{t}\right)^{\prime}=I_{m}+O\left(m/T\right)
.
Using it

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}g_{l}\left(\tau_{t}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\pi}_{l}^{\prime}\mathbb{B}^{m}\left(\tau_{t}\right)\mathbb{B}^{m}\left(\tau_{t}\right)^{\prime}\tilde{\pi}_{l}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\pi}_{l}^{\prime}\left[I_{m}+O\left(m/T\right)\right]\tilde{\pi}_{l}=\left\|\tilde{\pi}_{l}\right\|\left[1+o\left(1\right)\right],

due to
m/T\rightarrow 0
. Substituting the above result into (
H.1
)
yields the desired result by relaxing the lower and upper bounds to

\underline{C}_{xx}/2\left\|\tilde{\pi}_{l}\right\|
and

2\bar{C}_{xx}\left\|\tilde{\pi}_{l}\right\|
, respectively.
∎

Proof of Lemma
H.3
.

(i)
is standard in the literature. Lemma 5 in
Fan et al. (2011)

and Lemma S1.4 in
Su et al. (2024)
show a similar result for variables
with sub-exponential tails. We prove this lemma for
\text{E}\left(\left|x_{it}\right|^{q}\right)<\infty
.
Note that
\tilde{z}_{it}=\left[\mathbb{B}^{m}\left(\tau_{t}\right)^{\prime},\left(x_{it}\otimes\mathbb{B}^{m}\left(\tau_{t}\right)\right)^{\prime}\right]
,
a
\left[\left(p+1\right)m\right]\times 1
vector, then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\pi}^{\prime}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\tilde{\pi}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[\sum_{j=0}^{p}\sum_{l=0}^{p}\sum_{s=0}^{m-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{m-1}\pi_{js}\pi_{ls^{\prime}}B_{s}\left(\tau_{t}\right)B_{s^{\prime}}\left(\tau_{t}\right)x_{itj}x_{itl}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{p}\sum_{l=0}^{p}\sum_{s=0}^{m-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{m-1}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\pi_{js}\pi_{ls^{\prime}}B_{s}\left(\tau_{t}\right)B_{s^{\prime}}\left(\tau_{t}\right)x_{itj}x_{itl}\right]

where we let
x_{it0}
denote the constant 1. Using the above,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\pi}^{\prime}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\tilde{\pi}-\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\text{E}\left(\tilde{\pi}^{\prime}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\tilde{\pi}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{p}\sum_{l=0}^{p}\sum_{s=0}^{m-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{m-1}\pi_{js}\pi_{ls^{\prime}}\left\{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}B_{s}\left(\tau_{t}\right)B_{s^{\prime}}\left(\tau_{t}\right)\left[x_{itj}x_{itl}-\text{E}\left(x_{itj}x_{itl}\right)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{p}\sum_{l=0}^{p}\sum_{s=0}^{m-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{m-1}\pi_{js}\pi_{ls^{\prime}}I_{iss^{\prime}},

with

I_{iss^{\prime}}\equiv\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}B_{s}\left(\tau_{t}\right)B_{s^{\prime}}\left(\tau_{t}\right)\left[x_{itj}x_{itl}-\text{E}\left(x_{itj}x_{itl}\right)\right].

We first assume the event
\left\{\max_{i=1,...,N}\max_{s,s^{\prime}=1,...,m}\left|I_{iss^{\prime}}\right|\leq\frac{\epsilon}{mp^{2}}\right\}

holds; its probability bound will be shown later. Conditional on this
event, for all
\left\|\tilde{\pi}\right\|=1,

\displaystyle\max_{i=1,...,N}\left|\sum_{j=0}^{p}\sum_{l=0}^{p}\sum_{s=0}^{m-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{m-1}\pi_{js}\pi_{ls^{\prime}}I_{iss^{\prime}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\epsilon}{mp^{2}}\sum_{j=0}^{p}\sum_{l=0}^{p}\sum_{s=0}^{m-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{m-1}\left|\pi_{js}\pi_{ls^{\prime}}\right|.

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\epsilon}{mp^{2}}\sum_{j=0}^{p}\sum_{l=0}^{p}\sum_{s=0}^{m-1}\left[\left|\pi_{js}\right|\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{m-1}\left|\pi_{ls^{\prime}}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\epsilon}{mp^{2}}\sum_{j=0}^{p}\sum_{l=0}^{p}\sum_{s=0}^{m-1}\left|\pi_{js}\right|\left(\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{m-1}\pi_{ls^{\prime}}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{m-1}1^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{m}p^{2}}\sum_{j=0}^{p}\sum_{l=0}^{p}\sum_{s=0}^{m-1}\left|\pi_{js}\right|\leq\epsilon,

where the third and fourth lines hold by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
Thus

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,...,N}\sup_{\tilde{\pi}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\left(p+1\right)},\left\|\tilde{\pi}\right\|=1}\left|\sum_{j=0}^{p}\sum_{l=0}^{p}\sum_{s=0}^{m-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{m-1}\pi_{js}\pi_{ls^{\prime}}I_{iss^{\prime}}\right|\leq\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,...,N}\max_{s,s^{\prime}=1,...,m}\left|I_{iss^{\prime}}\right|\leq\frac{\epsilon}{mp^{2}}\right)

which is equivalent to

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,...,N}\sup_{\tilde{\pi}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\left(p+1\right)},\left\|\tilde{\pi}\right\|=1}\left|\sum_{j=0}^{p}\sum_{l=0}^{p}\sum_{s=0}^{m-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{m-1}\pi_{js}\pi_{ls^{\prime}}I_{iss^{\prime}}\right|>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,...,N}\max_{s,s^{\prime}=1,...,m}\left|I_{iss^{\prime}}\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{mp^{2}}\right).

(H.2)

Using the result in Lemma
H.1
(i) (by setting
\upsilon_{NT}=m
),
the moment condition on
x
(
C_{q}
in Lemma
H.1
is

q/2
in this case), and the fact that
p
is fixed, not hard to
see that

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,...,N}\max_{s,s^{\prime}=1,...,m}\left|I_{iss^{\prime}}\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{mp^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s,s^{\prime}=1}^{m}\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,...,N}\left|I_{iss^{\prime}}\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{p^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{s,s^{\prime}=1}^{m}\frac{NTm^{q/2}\left(\log N\right)^{2q}}{T^{q/2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{NTm^{q/2+2}\left(\log N\right)^{2q}}{T^{q/2}}.

Substitute it into (
H.2
), we obtain the desired result.

(ii)
is an immediate result from Lemmas
H.2

and the result (i).

Suppose the complement of the event in (i) holds with a small
\epsilon>0
.
For any
\tilde{\pi}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\left(p+1\right)}
and
\left\|\tilde{\pi}\right\|=1
,

\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\tilde{\pi}^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\right)\tilde{\pi}\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left(1+\epsilon\right)\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\text{E}\left(\tilde{\pi}^{\prime}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\tilde{\pi}\right)\leq 2\bar{C}_{xx}\left(1+\epsilon\right).

Similarly,

\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\tilde{\pi}^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\right)\tilde{\pi}\geq\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\left(1-\epsilon\right)\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\text{E}\left(\tilde{\pi}^{\prime}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\tilde{\pi}\right)\geq\frac{1}{2}\bar{C}_{xx}\left(1-\epsilon\right).

We show the result by setting
\underline{C}_{zz}=1/2\bar{C}_{xx}\left(1-\epsilon\right)

and
2\bar{C}_{xx}\left(1+\epsilon\right)
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
H.4
.

(i)

\tilde{z}_{it}v_{it}
is
(p+1)m
\times 1
. Elements
in
\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}v_{it}
are

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}x_{itj}B_{l}\left(\tau_{t}\right)v_{it},\text{ for }j=0,...,p\text{ and }l=0,...,m-1.

The idea is to obtain the probability bound of

\max_{i=1,...,N}\max_{j=0,...,p}\max_{l=0,...,m-1}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}x_{itj}B_{l}\left(\tau_{t}\right)v_{it}\right|>\epsilon/\sqrt{\left(p+1\right)m},

with which we can derive the probability bound of the event
\max_{i=1,...,N}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}v_{it}\right\|>\epsilon,

because the latter can be implied by the former. By the moment condition
in Assumption
2
, Lemma
H.1
(i) implies that
(by setting
\upsilon_{NT}=\sqrt{m}
and
C_{q}=q/2
)

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,...,N}\max_{j=0,...,p}\max_{l=0,...,m-1}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}x_{itj}B_{l}\left(\tau_{t}\right)v_{it}\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{\left(p+1\right)m}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{m-1}\sum_{j=0}^{p}\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,...,N}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}x_{itj}B_{l}\left(\tau_{t}\right)v_{it}\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{\left(p+1\right)m}}\right)\lesssim\frac{NTm^{q/4+1}\left(\log N\right)^{2q}}{T^{q/2}},

using the fact that
p
is a fixed constant. As a result

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,...,N}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}v_{it}\right\|>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,...,N}\max_{j=0,...,p}\max_{l=0,...,m-1}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}x_{itj}B_{l}\left(\tau_{t}\right)v_{it}\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{\left(p+1\right)m}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{NTm^{q/4+1}\left(\log N\right)^{2q}}{T^{q/2}}.

(ii)
Similarly,
\tilde{z}_{it}\xi_{it}
is
(p+1)m
\times 1
.
Elements in
\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}\xi_{it}
are

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}x_{itj}B_{l}\left(\tau_{t}\right)\xi_{it}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{p}x_{itj}x_{itj^{\prime}}B_{l}\left(\tau_{t}\right)b_{ij^{\prime}}\left(\tau_{t}\right)

\text{ for }j=0,1,...,p\text{ and }l=0,1,...,m-1
. By the condition

\kappa\geq 1
in Assumption
4
, so that
m^{-\kappa}\ll m^{-1/2}
.
Using Lemma
H.1
(ii),

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,...,N}\max_{j=0,...,p}\max_{l=0,...,m-1}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{p}x_{itj}x_{itj^{\prime}}B_{l}\left(\tau_{t}\right)b_{ij^{\prime}}\left(\tau_{t}\right)\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{\left(p+1\right)m}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{p}\sum_{l=0}^{m-1}\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{p}\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,...,N}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}x_{itj}x_{itj^{\prime}}B_{l}\left(\tau_{t}\right)b_{ij^{\prime}}\left(\tau_{t}\right)\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{\left(p+1\right)^{3/2}\sqrt{m}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{p}\sum_{l=0}^{m-1}\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{p}\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,...,N}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}x_{itj}x_{itj^{\prime}}B_{l}\left(\tau_{t}\right)b_{ij^{\prime}}\left(\tau_{t}\right)\right|>Mm^{-\kappa}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{NTm\left(\log N\right)^{2q}}{T^{q/2}}.

for an arbitrary large
M
after some large
T,
where the third
line uses
m^{-\kappa}\ll m^{-1/2}
and the last line holds by applying
Lemma
H.1
(ii) with
C_{q}=q/2
and the fact that
p

is fixed.

We reach the desired result by

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,...,N}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}\xi_{it}\right\|>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,...,N}\max_{l=0,...,m}\max_{j=0,...,p}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{p}x_{itj}x_{itj^{\prime}}B_{l}\left(\tau_{t}\right)b_{ij^{\prime}}\left(\tau_{t}\right)\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{\left(p+1\right)m}}\right).

∎

Proof of Lemma
H.5
.

(i)
By (
2.6
) and (
E.3
), we
substitute
y_{it}=\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\tilde{\pi}_{i}^{0}+\xi_{it}+v_{it}

into
\widehat{\tilde{\pi}}_{i}
, and obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{\tilde{\pi}}_{i}

\displaystyle-\tilde{\pi}_{i}^{0}=\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}\xi_{it}\right)+\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}v_{it}\right).

Then

\left\|\widehat{\tilde{\pi}}_{i}-\tilde{\pi}_{i}^{0}\right\|\leq\mu_{\min}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\right)\left[\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}\xi_{it}\right\|+\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}v_{it}\right\|\right],

which implies

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left\|\widehat{\tilde{\pi}}_{i}-\tilde{\pi}_{i}^{0}\right\|>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\mu_{\min}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\right)>C_{zz}^{-1}\right)+\Pr\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}\xi_{it}\right\|+\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}v_{it}\right\|>C_{zz}\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\mu_{\min}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\right)>C_{zz}^{-1}\right)+\Pr\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}\xi_{it}\right\|>\frac{C_{zz}\epsilon}{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\Pr\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}v_{it}\right\|>\frac{C_{zz}\epsilon}{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o\left(1\right),

by Lemmas
H.3
and
H.4
, and
the rates in Assumption
6
(ii).

(ii)
Note that

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{vi}^{2}

\displaystyle-\sigma_{vi}^{2}=\frac{1}{T-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(y_{it}-\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\widehat{\tilde{\pi}}_{i}\right)^{2}-\sigma_{vi}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\left(\tilde{\pi}_{i}^{0}-\widehat{\tilde{\pi}}_{i}\right)+\xi_{it}+v_{it}\right)^{2}-\sigma_{vi}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}v_{it}^{2}-\sigma_{vi}^{2}+\left(\tilde{\pi}_{i}^{0}-\widehat{\tilde{\pi}}_{i}\right)^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{T-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{z}_{it}\tilde{z}_{it}^{\prime}\right)\left(\tilde{\pi}_{i}^{0}-\widehat{\tilde{\pi}}_{i}\right)+\frac{1}{T-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}^{2}

\displaystyle+2\left[\frac{1}{T-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\xi_{it}+v_{it}\right)z_{it}^{\prime}\right]\left(\tilde{\pi}_{i}^{0}-\widehat{\tilde{\pi}}_{i}\right)+2\frac{1}{T-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}v_{it}

\displaystyle\equiv A_{i1}+A_{i2}+A_{i3}+A_{i4}+A_{i5}.

The uniform convergence of
A_{i1},A_{i2},A_{i3},A_{i4},\text{ and }A_{i5}

can be similarly shown as in Lemmas
H.1
,
H.3
,

H.4
and part (i) of this lemma. We omit the proof
for conciseness. Therefore

\Pr\left(\max_{i=1,...,N}\left|\hat{\sigma}_{vi}^{2}-\sigma_{vi}^{2}\right|>\epsilon\right)=o\left(1\right).

∎

Proof of Lemma
H.6
.

Recall that Lemma
H.2
shows that

\frac{\underline{C}_{xx}}{2}\leq\mu_{\min}\left\{\text{E}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\underline{z}}_{it}\tilde{\underline{z}}_{it}^{\prime}\right)\right\}\leq\mu_{\max}\left\{\text{E}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\underline{z}}_{it}\tilde{\underline{z}}_{it}^{\prime}\right)\right\}\leq 2\bar{C}_{xx},

after some large
T.
Note
\tilde{z}_{it}=\left(1,z_{it}^{\prime}\right)^{\prime}.

Thus,

\text{E}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\underline{z}}_{it}\tilde{\underline{z}}_{it}^{\prime}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&\text{E}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\underline{z}_{it}^{\prime}\right)\\
\text{E}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\underline{z}_{it}\right)&\text{E}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\underline{z}_{it}\underline{z}_{it}^{\prime}\right)\end{array}\right).

With the block representation of
\text{E}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\underline{z}}_{it}\tilde{\underline{z}}_{it}^{\prime}\right)

and its full rank condition, the inverse of it can be calculated as
(we only present its lower diagonal):

\displaystyle\left[\text{E}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\underline{z}}_{it}\tilde{\underline{z}}_{it}^{\prime}\right)\right]^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\cdot_{1\times 1}&\cdot_{1\times\left(mp+m-1\right)}\\
\cdot_{\left(mp+m-1\right)\times 1}&\left[\text{E}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\underline{z}_{it}\underline{z}_{it}^{\prime}\right)-\text{E}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\underline{z}_{it}\right)\text{E}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\underline{z}_{it}^{\prime}\right)\right]^{-1}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\cdot_{1\times 1}&\cdot_{1\times\left(mp+m-1\right)}\\
\cdot_{\left(mp+m-1\right)\times 1}&\left[\text{E}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{\underline{z}}_{it}\ddot{\underline{z}}_{it}^{\prime}\right)\right]^{-1}\end{array}\right).

The above implies that
\text{E}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{z}_{it}\ddot{z}_{it}^{\prime}\right)

must be of full rank, and

\frac{\bar{C}_{xx}^{-1}}{2}\leq\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\mu_{\min}\left\{\left[\text{E}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}^{\prime}\right)\right]^{-1}\right\}\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\mu_{\max}\left\{\left[\text{E}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}^{\prime}\right)\right]^{-1}\right\}\leq 2\underline{C}_{xx}^{-1}.

We obtain the desired result by
\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\mu_{\min}\left(A_{i}^{-1}\right)=1\left/\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\mu_{\max}\left(A_{i}\right)\right.

for any full rank matrices
\left\{A_{i}\right\}_{i=1}^{N}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
H.7
.

The proof here follows that of Lemma
H.3
.
We similarly define

I_{ss^{\prime}\left(k\right)}\equiv\frac{1}{N_{k}}\sum_{i\in G_{k|K^{*}}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{B_{s}\left(\tau_{t}\right)B_{s^{\prime}}\left(\tau_{t}\right)\left[x_{itj}x_{itl}-\text{E}\left(x_{itj}x_{itl}\right)\right]\right\}.

Its probability bound is

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{s,s^{\prime}=1,...,\underline{m}}\left|I_{ss^{\prime}\left(k\right)}\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{\underline{m}p^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s,s^{\prime}=1}^{\underline{m}}\Pr\left(\left|I_{ss^{\prime}\left(k\right)}\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{\underline{m}p^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\underline{m}^{q/2+2}\left(\log N\right)^{2q}}{\left(NT\right)^{q/2-1}},

where we use a similar argument as in the proof of (i) in Lemma
H.1

and we set
\upsilon_{NT}=\underline{m}p^{2}
,
C_{NT}=\upsilon_{NT}^{-1}NT\left(\log N\right)^{-4}
,
in addition, we use the assumptions that
T\propto N^{C}
and
N_{k}\propto N
.

The desired result follows, using the same logic as in the proof of
(i) in Lemma
H.1
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
H.8
.

To analyze
A_{k1}
, we introduce some new notation:

\ddot{\underline{Z}}_{\left(k\right)}\equiv\left(\underline{\ddot{z}}_{11},...,\underline{\ddot{z}}_{1T},....,\underline{\ddot{z}}_{N_{k}1},...,\underline{\ddot{z}}_{N_{k}T}\right)^{\prime},

a
N_{k}T\times\left(\underline{m}-1+\underline{m}p\right)
matrix,
where we abuse the notation by letting
\underline{\ddot{z}}_{i1}

in
\ddot{\underline{Z}}_{\left(k\right)}
denote all observations
in
G_{k|K}
. Similarly

\ddot{\xi}_{\left(k\right)}\equiv\left(\ddot{\xi}_{11},...,\ddot{\xi}_{1T},...,\ddot{\xi}_{N_{k}1},...,\ddot{\xi}_{N_{k}T}\right)^{\prime},

a
N_{k}T\times 1
vector.

By Lemmas
H.6

and
H.7

on

\frac{1}{N_{k}T}\ddot{Z}_{\left(k\right)}^{\prime}\ddot{Z}_{\left(k\right)},

with

very probability,

\mu_{\min}\left(\frac{1}{N_{k}T}\ddot{Z}_{\left(k\right)}^{\prime}\ddot{Z}_{\left(k\right)}\right)=\mu_{\min}\left(\frac{1}{N_{k}T}\sum_{i\in G_{k|K^{*}}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}^{\prime}\right)\geq\frac{\underline{C}_{xx}}{2}-\epsilon\geq\frac{\underline{C}_{xx}}{3},

(H.3)

by setting a small enough
\epsilon
, and similarly

\mu_{\max}\left(\frac{1}{N_{k}T}\ddot{Z}_{\left(k\right)}^{\prime}\ddot{Z}_{\left(k\right)}\right)\leq 3\bar{C}_{xx}.

Thus, with very high probability,

\displaystyle\left\|A_{k1}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{1}{N_{k}T}\ddot{\xi}_{\left(k\right)}^{\prime}\ddot{Z}_{\left(k\right)}\left(\frac{1}{N_{k}T}\ddot{Z}_{\left(k\right)}^{\prime}\ddot{Z}_{\left(k\right)}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{N_{k}T}\ddot{Z}_{\left(k\right)}^{\prime}\ddot{Z}_{\left(k\right)}\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{N_{k}T}\ddot{Z}_{\left(k\right)}^{\prime}\ddot{\xi}_{\left(k\right)}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq 3\underline{C}_{xx}^{-1}\left[\frac{1}{N_{k}T}\ddot{\xi}_{\left(k\right)}^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{N_{k}T}\ddot{Z}_{\left(k\right)}\ddot{Z}_{\left(k\right)}^{\prime}\right)\ddot{\xi}_{\left(k\right)}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq 3^{3/2}\underline{C}_{xx}^{-1}\bar{C}_{xx}^{1/2}\left[\frac{1}{N_{k}T}\ddot{\xi}_{\left(k\right)}^{\prime}\ddot{\xi}_{\left(k\right)}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle=3^{3/2}\underline{C}_{xx}^{-1}\bar{C}_{xx}^{1/2}\left[\frac{1}{N_{k}T}\sum_{i\in G_{k|K}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{\xi}_{it}^{2}\right]^{1/2}.

(H.4)

By the rate in (
E.5
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{k}T}\sum_{i\in G_{k|K}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{\xi}_{it}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\frac{1}{N_{k}T}\sum_{i\in G_{k|K}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(1+\sum_{l=1}^{p}\left|x_{itl}\right|\right)^{2}\right]\cdot O\left(m^{-2\kappa}\right)

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(m^{-2\kappa}\right),

(H.5)

where the second line holds by the moment condition in Assumption

2
. Substitute (
H.5
) back to (
H.4
),
and we obtain the desired result.
∎

Proof of Lemma
H.9
.

Recall that

\mathbb{M_{B}}\left(s\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\mathbb{B}_{-0}^{\underline{m}}\left(s\right)^{\prime}&0&\cdots&0\\
0&\mathbb{B}^{\underline{m}}\left(s\right)^{\prime}&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&0&\cdots&\mathbb{B}^{\underline{m}}\left(s\right)^{\prime}\end{array}\right)_{\left(p+1\right)\times\left(\underline{m}-1+\underline{m}p\right)},

and

Q_{(k),zz}=\frac{1}{N_{k}T}\sum_{i\in G_{k|K}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}^{\prime}.

For any
\left(p+1\right)\times 1
vector
a
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{N_{k}T}{\underline{m}}}a^{\prime}\mathbb{M_{B}}\left(s\right)A_{k2}

\displaystyle=a^{\prime}\mathbb{M_{B}}\left(s\right)\left(\frac{1}{N_{k}T}\sum_{i\in G_{k|K}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{k}T\underline{m}}}\sum_{i\in G_{k|K}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}\ddot{v}_{it}\right)

\displaystyle=a^{\prime}\mathbb{M_{B}}\left(s\right)Q_{(k),zz}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{k}T\underline{m}}}\sum_{i\in G_{k|K}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}v_{it}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{k}}}\sum_{i\in G_{k|K}}\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{T\underline{m}}}a^{\prime}\mathbb{M_{B}}\left(s\right)Q_{(k),zz}^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}v_{it}\right\},

(H.6)

where the second line uses
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}\ddot{v}_{it}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}v_{it}
.
By the i.i.d. assumption across
i
and
t
on
v_{it}
and its
independence with
x,
the conditional variance of this term can
be calculated as

\text{Var}\left(\left.\sqrt{\frac{N_{k}T}{\underline{m}}}a^{\prime}\mathbb{M_{B}}\left(s\right)A_{k2}\right|x_{1},...,x_{N}\right)=a^{\prime}\left[\frac{\sigma_{v\left(k\right)}^{2}}{\underline{m}}\mathbb{M_{B}}\left(s\right)Q_{(k),zz}^{-1}\mathbb{M_{B}}\left(s\right)^{\prime}\right]a.

(H.7)

The above is finite and proportional to
\left\|a\right\|^{2}.

To see it, (
H.3
) implies that with very high probability

\displaystyle\text{Var}\left(\left.a^{\prime}\mathbb{M_{B}}\left(s\right)A_{k2}\right|x_{1},...,x_{N}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 3\underline{C}_{xx}^{-1}\frac{\sigma_{v\left(k\right)}^{2}}{\underline{m}}a^{\prime}\mathbb{M_{B}}\left(s\right)\mathbb{M_{B}}\left(s\right)^{\prime}a

\displaystyle=3\underline{C}_{xx}^{-1}\sigma_{v\left(k\right)}^{2}\left[\frac{1}{\underline{m}}\mathbb{B}_{-0}^{\underline{m}}\left(s\right)^{\prime}\mathbb{B}_{-0}^{\underline{m}}\left(s\right)a_{0}^{2}+\frac{1}{\underline{m}}\mathbb{B}^{\underline{m}}\left(s\right)^{\prime}\mathbb{B}^{\underline{m}}\left(s\right)\sum_{l=1}^{p}a_{l}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\propto\sum_{l=0}^{p}a_{l}^{2}=\left\|a\right\|^{2},

where we abuse the notation a bit by letting
a=\left(a_{0},a_{1},...,a_{p}\right)^{\prime}
.
We can similarly verify the Lindeberg condition for the last term
in (
H.6
) as in Lemma A.8 in
Huang et al. (2004)
or
Lemma A.8 in
Su et al. (2019)
. We omit this verification process
due to the similarity. We then apply the Lindeberg Central Limit Theorem,
and obtain

\left.\sqrt{\frac{N_{k}T}{\underline{m}}}a^{\prime}\mathbb{M_{B}}\left(s\right)A_{k2}\right/\left\{a^{\prime}\left[\frac{\sigma_{v\left(k\right)}^{2}}{\underline{m}}\mathbb{M_{B}}\left(s\right)Q_{(k),zz}^{-1}\mathbb{M_{B}}\left(s\right)^{\prime}\right]a\right\}^{1/2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}N\left(0,1\right).

∎

Proof of Lemma
H.10
.

We only show the result that

\Pr\left(\text{IC}\left(K^{*},\lambda_{NT}\right)<\text{IC}\left(K^{*}-1,\lambda_{NT}\right)\right)\rightarrow 1.

Other cases are similar. Define an event

\mathcal{M}_{1}=\left\{\text{Two groups merged and other groups corrected classified}\right\}.

By the nature of the HAC algorithm, and the proof in Theorem
3.1
,
we can similarly show that

\Pr\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}\right)\rightarrow 1.

Recall that

\mathcal{M}\equiv\left\{\left(\hat{G}_{1|K^{*}},\hat{G}_{2|K^{*}},\ldots,\hat{G}_{K^{*}|K^{*}}\right)=\left(G_{1|K^{*}},G_{2|K^{*}},\ldots,G_{K^{*}|K^{*}}\right)\right\}.

We first show the result conditional on
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{M}_{1},

then we show the result unconditionally. Without loss of generality,
assume that observations in group
K^{*}-1
and
K^{*}
are merged.
Thus,

\displaystyle\frac{\text{IC}\left(K^{*},\lambda_{NT}\right)-\text{IC}\left(K^{*}-1,\lambda_{NT}\right)}{\left(N_{K^{*}-1}+N_{K^{*}}\right)T}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\left(N_{K^{*}-1}+N_{K^{*}}\right)T}\left\{\sum_{k=K^{*}-1}^{K^{*}}N_{k}T\log\left(\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(k|K^{*}\right)}\right)+\lambda_{NT}-\left(N_{K^{*}-1}+N_{K^{*}}\right)T\log\left(\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}-1\right)}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{N_{K^{*}-1}}{N_{K^{*}-1}+N_{K^{*}}}\log\left(\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}\right)}\right)+\frac{N_{K^{*}}}{N_{K^{*}-1}+N_{K^{*}}}\log\left(\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}|K^{*}\right)}\right)-\log\left(\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}-1\right)}\right)+o\left(1\right),

(H.8)

where the last line uses
\lambda_{NT}=o\left(NT\right)
and
N_{k}\propto N.

We claim the result below and we defer its proof to the end:

\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}-1\right)}^{2}=\frac{N_{K^{*}-1}}{N_{K^{*}-1}+N_{K^{*}}}\left(\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}\right)}^{2}+\Delta_{1}^{2}\right)+\frac{N_{K^{*}}}{N_{K^{*}-1}+N_{K^{*}}}\left(\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}|K^{*}\right)}^{2}+\Delta_{2}^{2}\right)+o_{P}\left(1\right),

(H.9)

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{N_{K^{*}-1}T}\sum_{i\in G_{K^{*}-1|K^{*}}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}^{\prime}\left(\pi_{\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}-1\right)}^{0*}-\pi_{\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}\right)}^{0*}\right)\right]^{2},\text{ and}

\displaystyle\Delta_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{N_{K^{*}}T}\sum_{i\in G_{K^{*}|K^{*}}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}^{\prime}\left(\pi_{\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}-1\right)}^{0*}-\pi_{\left(K^{*}|K^{*}\right)}^{0*}\right)\right]^{2},

and
\pi
0∗
{}_{\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}-1\right)}
denotes the estimand
of
\pi
for
G_{K^{*}-1|K^{*}}\cup G_{K^{*}|K^{*}}
.

Using (
H.9
) and the Jensen’s inequality,

\displaystyle\log\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}-1\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{N_{K^{*}-1}}{N_{K^{*}-1}+N_{K^{*}}}\log\left(\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}\right)}^{2}+\Delta_{1}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{N_{K^{*}}}{N_{K^{*}-1}+N_{K^{*}}}\log\left(\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}|K^{*}\right)}^{2}+\Delta_{2}^{2}\right)+o_{P}\left(1\right).

(H.10)

Since

\left\{\alpha_{\left(K^{*}-1\right)}^{*},\beta_{\left(K^{*}-1\right)}^{\ast},\sigma_{v\left(K^{*}-1\right)}^{\ast 2}\right\}\neq\left\{\alpha_{\left(K^{*}\right)}^{*},\beta_{\left(K^{*}\right)}^{\ast},\sigma_{v\left(K^{*}\right)}^{\ast 2}\right\},

we either have
\sigma_{v\left(K^{*}-1\right)}^{\ast 2}\neq\sigma_{v\left(K^{*}\right)}^{\ast 2}

or
\left\{\alpha_{\left(K^{*}-1\right)}^{*},\beta_{\left(K^{*}-1\right)}^{\ast}\right\}\neq\left\{\alpha_{\left(K^{*}\right)}^{*},\beta_{\left(K^{*}\right)}^{\ast}\right\}
,
so that either
\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}\right)}^{2}\neq\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}|K^{*}\right)}^{2}

or
\max\left\{\Delta_{1}^{2},\Delta_{2}^{2}\right\}>0
holds with
very high probability. Together with this, we can change “
\geq
”
in (
H.10
) to “
>
”, so that

\log\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}-1\right)}^{2}>\frac{N_{K^{*}-1}}{N_{K^{*}-1}+N_{K^{*}}}\log\left(\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}\right)}^{2}\right)+\frac{N_{K^{*}}}{N_{K^{*}-1}+N_{K^{*}}}\log\left(\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}|K^{*}\right)}^{2}\right)+o_{P}\left(1\right)

(H.11)

Using (
H.11
), (
H.8
) implies that

\text{IC}\left(K^{*},\lambda_{NT}\right)-\text{IC}\left(K^{*}-1,\lambda_{NT}\right)<0

holds with very high probability after some large
N
and
T
.

We have shown the desired result conditional on
\mathcal{M}
and

\mathcal{M}_{1}
. Since

\Pr\left(\mathcal{M}\cap\mathcal{M}_{1}\right)\geq 1-\Pr\left(\mathcal{M}^{c}\right)-\Pr\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}^{c}\right)\rightarrow 1,

the desired result then holds unconditionally.

We finish the proof by showing the claim in (
H.9
).
Then as in the proof of (ii) in Theorem
3.2

(the decomposition of
\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(k|K^{*}\right)}^{2}
),
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in G_{K^{*}-1|K^{*}}\cup G_{K^{*}|K^{*}}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\ddot{y}_{it}-\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}^{\prime}\hat{\pi}_{\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}-1\right)}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in G_{K^{*}-1|K^{*}}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}^{\prime}\left(\hat{\pi}_{\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}-1\right)}-\pi_{\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}-1\right)}^{0*}\right)+\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}^{\prime}\left(\pi_{\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}-1\right)}^{0*}-\pi_{\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}\right)}^{0*}\right)+\ddot{\xi}_{it}+\ddot{v}_{it}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i\in\cup G_{K^{*}|K^{*}}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}^{\prime}\left(\hat{\pi}_{\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}-1\right)}-\pi_{\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}-1\right)}^{0*}\right)+\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}^{\prime}\left(\pi_{\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}-1\right)}^{0*}-\pi_{\left(K^{*}|K^{*}\right)}^{0*}\right)+\ddot{\xi}_{it}+\ddot{v}_{it}\right]^{2}.

Using the decomposition above and by the same analysis for
\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(k|K^{*}\right)}^{2}

in the proof of (ii) in Theorem
3.2
, we can
extract the leading term of the following:

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}-1\right)}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left(N_{K^{*}-1}+N_{K^{*}}\right)\left(T-1\right)}\sum_{i\in G_{K^{*}-1|K^{*}}\cup G_{K^{*}|K^{*}}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\ddot{y}_{it}-\underline{\ddot{z}}_{it}^{\prime}\hat{\pi}_{\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}-1\right)}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left(N_{K^{*}-1}+N_{K^{*}}\right)\left(T-1\right)}\sum_{i\in G_{K^{*}-1|K^{*}}}\sum_{i\in G_{K^{*}|K^{*}}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{v}_{it}^{2}+\Delta_{1}^{2}+\Delta_{2}^{2}+o_{P}\left(1\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{N_{K^{*}-1}}{N_{K^{*}-1}+N_{K^{*}}}\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}-1|K^{*}\right)}^{2}+\Delta_{1}^{2}+\frac{N_{K^{*}}}{N_{K^{*}-1}+N_{K^{*}}}\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}|K^{*}\right)}^{2}+\Delta_{2}^{2}+o_{P}\left(1\right).

where the second and third lines repeatedly use the arguments for
the analysis of
\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(k|K^{*}\right)}^{2}
. The above
is the desired result.
∎

Proof of Lemma
H.11
.

We only show the result for
K=K^{*}+1.
Other cases are similar.
Define an event

\mathcal{M}_{2}=\left\{\text{One group separated into two groups and other groups corrected classified}\right\}.

By the nature of the HAC algorithm, and the proof in Theorem
3.1
,
we can similarly show that

\Pr\left(\mathcal{M}_{2}\right)\rightarrow 1.

We show the result conditional on
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}.

Without loss of generality, assume that observations in group
K^{*}

are separated into two groups, and the numbers of observations in
groups
K^{*}
and
K^{*}+1
are denoted as
N_{K^{*}1}
and
N_{K^{*}2}
,
respectively. Clearly
N_{K^{*}}=N_{K^{*}1}+N_{K^{*}2}
. Since these
two groups come from the same underlying group, the parameters of
interest are the same. Using the rates in Theorem
3.2
,

\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}|K^{*}+1\right)}^{2}-\sigma_{v\left(K^{*}\right)}^{*2}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{K^{*}1}T}}\right)\text{ and }\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}+1|K^{*}+1\right)}^{2}-\sigma_{v\left(K^{*}\right)}^{*2}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{K^{*}2}T}}\right).

Since
\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}|K^{*}\right)}^{2}-\sigma_{v\left(K^{*}\right)}^{*2}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{K^{*}}T}}\right),

with the above, we have

\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}|K^{*}+1\right)}^{2}-\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}|K^{*}\right)}^{2}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{K^{*}1}T}}\right)\text{ and }\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}+1|K^{*}+1\right)}^{2}-\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}|K^{*}\right)}^{2}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{K^{*}2}T}}\right)

Then

\displaystyle\frac{\text{IC}\left(K^{*}+1,\lambda_{NT}\right)-\text{IC}\left(K^{*},\lambda_{NT}\right)}{N_{K^{*}}T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{K^{*}}T}\left\{N_{K^{*}1}T\log\left(\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}|K^{*}+1\right)}\right)+N_{K^{*}2}T\log\left(\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}+1|K^{*}+1\right)}\right)+\lambda_{NT}\right.\left.-N_{k}T\log\left(\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}|K^{*}\right)}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{N_{K^{*}1}}{N_{K^{*}}}\left[\log\left(\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}|K^{*}+1\right)}^{2}\right)-\log\left(\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}|K^{*}\right)}^{2}\right)\right]+\frac{N_{K^{*}2}}{N_{K^{*}}}\left[\log\left(\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}+1|K^{*}+1\right)}^{2}\right)-\log\left(\hat{\sigma}_{v\left(K^{*}|K^{*}\right)}^{2}\right)\right]+\frac{\lambda_{NT}}{N_{K^{*}}T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{N_{K^{*}1}}{N_{K^{*}}}O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{K^{*}1}T}}\right)+\frac{N_{K^{*}2}}{N_{K^{*}}}O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{K^{*}2}T}}\right)+\frac{\lambda_{NT}}{N_{K^{*}}T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sqrt{\frac{N_{K^{*}1}}{N_{K^{*}}}}+\sqrt{\frac{N_{K^{*}2}}{N_{K^{*}}}}\right)\cdot O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{K^{*}}T}}\right)+\frac{\lambda_{NT}}{N_{K^{*}}T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{K^{*}}T}}\right)+\frac{\lambda_{NT}}{N_{K^{*}}T}>0,

with very high probability, where the last line holds due to
\sqrt{\frac{N_{K^{*}1}}{N_{K^{*}}}}+\sqrt{\frac{N_{K^{*}2}}{N_{K^{*}}}}\leq 2
,

\lambda_{NT}\gg
\sqrt{NT},
and
N_{K^{*}}\propto N.

We have shown the desired result conditional on
\mathcal{M}
and

\mathcal{M}_{2}
. Since

\Pr\left(\mathcal{M}\cap\mathcal{M}_{2}\right)\geq 1-\Pr\left(\mathcal{M}^{c}\right)-\Pr\left(\mathcal{M}_{2}^{c}\right)\rightarrow 1,

the desired result then holds unconditionally.
∎

Proof of Lemma
H.12
.

Without loss of generality, we ignore the group structure of other
parameters for the following discussion. Moreover, we assume that
we know
\left(\alpha(\cdot),\beta(\cdot),\sigma_{v}^{2}\right)
.
It is innocuous because
\hat{\vartheta}
converges to the true value
faster than
\sqrt{N}
which is the convergence rate of
\left(\hat{\alpha}^{0},\hat{\sigma}_{u}^{2}\right)
.

As in Lemmas
H.10
and
H.11
, we only show that

\Pr\left(\widetilde{\mathrm{IC}}\left(\mathcal{K}^{*},\tilde{\lambda}_{NT}\right)<\widetilde{\mathrm{IC}}\left(\mathcal{K}^{*}-1,\tilde{\lambda}_{NT}\right)\right)\rightarrow 1,

and

\Pr\left(\widetilde{\mathrm{IC}}\left(\mathcal{K}^{*},\tilde{\lambda}_{NT}\right)<\widetilde{\mathrm{IC}}\left(\mathcal{K}^{*}+1,\tilde{\lambda}_{NT}\right)\right)\rightarrow 1.

As such, we divide the proof into two parts with Part 1 showing the
first result and Part 2 for the other.

Part 1.
We show in Appendix
G.2
that
the information matrix is a well-behaved positive definite matrix
with diagonals being finite constants, even though
T\rightarrow\infty.

Recall that

\varrho^{0}=\left(\alpha_{(1)}^{0},\sigma_{u(1)}^{2},...,\alpha_{(\mathcal{K^{*}})}^{0},\sigma_{u(\mathcal{K}^{*})}^{2},\tau_{1}^{0},...,\tau_{\mathcal{\mathcal{K}^{*}}-1}^{0}\right).

The true density function for
y
is

\tilde{f}\left(y\left|x;\varrho^{0},\vartheta\right.\right),

where we abuse the notation by letting
\vartheta\equiv\left(\sigma_{v}^{2},\alpha(\cdot),\beta(\cdot)\right)
.
By definition,
\varrho^{0}
uniquely maximizes

\log\left[\tilde{f}\left(y\left|x;\varrho,\vartheta\right.\right)\right].

For a
\varrho
that is in a small neighborhood of
\varrho^{0},

\log\left[\tilde{f}\left(y\left|x;\varrho,\vartheta\right.\right)\right]-\log\left[\tilde{f}\left(y\left|x;\varrho^{0},\vartheta\right.\right)\right]=-\left(\varrho-\varrho^{0}\right)^{\prime}\mathbb{I}\left(\varrho-\varrho^{0}\right)+o\left(\left\|\varrho-\varrho^{0}\right\|^{2}\right).

As mentioned at the beginning,
\mathbb{I}
is positive definite.
Further, we restrict our attention to a compact support of
\varrho,

therefore, there exists a
C
such that

\log\left[\tilde{f}\left(y\left|x;\varrho,\vartheta\right.\right)\right]-\log\left[\tilde{f}\left(y\left|x;\varrho^{0},\vartheta\right.\right)\right]\leq-C<0,

(H.12)

for all
\varrho
outside of a small neighborhood of
\varrho^{0}.

When we restrict the mixture distribution to come from
\mathcal{K}^{*}-1

distributions, clearly, the estimate converges to a parameter that
is outside the small neighborhood of
\varrho^{0}.
By the consistency
and inequality in (
H.12
),

\tilde{\text{IC}}\left(\mathcal{K}^{*},\tilde{\lambda}_{NT}\right)-\tilde{\text{IC}}\left(\mathcal{K}^{*}-1,\tilde{\lambda}_{NT}\right)\leq-C\cdot N+o_{P}\left(N\right)+\tilde{\lambda}_{NT}.

Further,
\tilde{\lambda}_{NT}\ll N,
the above implies the first
desired result.

Part 2.
When we force
\mathcal{K}=\mathcal{K}^{*}+1,

denote

\varsigma^{0}=\left(\alpha_{(1)}^{0},\sigma_{u(1)}^{2},...,\alpha_{(\mathcal{K^{*}}+1)}^{0},\sigma_{u(\mathcal{K}^{*}+1)}^{2},\tau_{1}^{0},...,\tau_{\mathcal{\mathcal{K}^{*}}}^{0}\right),

as the population parameter. It is not unique but we use the one that
the estimated parameters converge to.
\varsigma^{0}
is essentially
the same as
\varrho^{0}
in terms of mixture distribution; mathematically,

\tilde{f}\left(y_{i}\left|x_{i};\varsigma^{0},\vartheta\right.\right)=\tilde{f}\left(y_{i}\left|x_{i};\varrho^{0},\vartheta\right.\right),

(H.13)

where as before
\vartheta=\left(\sigma_{v}^{2},\alpha(\cdot),\beta(\cdot)\right)
.
To see that, on the one hand

\textrm{E}\left[\log\tilde{f}\left(y_{i}\left|x_{i};\varsigma^{0},\vartheta\right.\right)\right]\geq\textrm{E}\left[\log\tilde{f}\left(y_{i}\left|x_{i};\varrho^{0},\vartheta\right.\right)\right],

because
\varsigma^{0}
has more degrees of freedom than that of

\varrho^{0}
. On the other hand,

\textrm{E}\left[\log\tilde{f}\left(y_{i}\left|x_{i};\varsigma^{0},\vartheta\right.\right)\right]\leq\textrm{E}\left[\log\tilde{f}\left(y_{i}\left|x_{i};\varrho^{0},\vartheta\right.\right)\right],

because
\tilde{f}\left(y_{i}\left|x_{i};\varrho^{0},\vartheta\right.\right)

is the true underlying distribution. Therefore, (
H.13
)
must hold.

Let
\hat{\varsigma}
be the estimates when
\mathcal{K}=\mathcal{K}^{*}+1
.
Then, using (
H.13
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\log\tilde{f}\left(y_{i}\left|x_{i};\hat{\varsigma},\vartheta\right.\right)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\log\tilde{f}\left(y_{i}\left|x_{i};\hat{\varrho},\vartheta\right.\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left[\log\tilde{f}\left(y_{i}\left|x_{i};\hat{\varsigma},\vartheta\right.\right)-\log\tilde{f}\left(y_{i}\left|x_{i};\varsigma^{0},\vartheta\right.\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left[\log\tilde{f}\left(y_{i}\left|x_{i};\hat{\varrho},\vartheta\right.\right)-\log\tilde{f}\left(y_{i}\left|x_{i};\varrho^{0},\vartheta\right.\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varsigma}\log\tilde{f}_{i}\right|_{\varsigma=\varsigma^{0}}\right)^{\prime}\left(\hat{\varsigma}-\varsigma^{0}\right)+O_{P}\left(\left\|\hat{\varsigma}-\varsigma^{0}\right\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varrho}\log\tilde{f_{i}}\right|_{\varrho=\varrho^{0}}\right)^{\prime}\left(\hat{\varrho}-\varrho^{0}\right)+O_{P}\left(\left\|\hat{\varrho}-\varrho^{0}\right\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}\left(N^{-1}\right),

where the last line holds by the first order condition and the independence
across
N
so that

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varsigma}\log f_{i}\right|_{\varsigma=\varsigma^{0}}=O_{P}\left(N^{-1/2}\right)\text{ and }\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varrho}\log\tilde{f_{i}}\right|_{\varrho=\varrho^{0}}=O_{P}\left(N^{-1/2}\right),

\left\|\hat{\varsigma}-\varsigma^{0}\right\|=O_{P}\left(N^{-1/2}\right)
,
and
\left\|\hat{\varrho}-\varrho^{0}\right\|=O_{P}\left(N^{-1/2}\right)
.
Using the above,

\widetilde{\mathrm{IC}}\left(\mathcal{K}^{*},\tilde{\lambda}_{NT}\right)-\widetilde{\mathrm{IC}}\left(\mathcal{K}^{*}+1,\tilde{\lambda}_{NT}\right)=-\tilde{\lambda}_{NT}+O_{P}\left(1\right).

Then

\Pr\left(\widetilde{\mathrm{IC}}\left(\mathcal{K}^{*},\tilde{\lambda}_{NT}\right)<\widetilde{\mathrm{IC}}\left(\mathcal{K}^{*}+1,\tilde{\lambda}_{NT}\right)\right)\rightarrow 1,

due to
\tilde{\lambda}_{NT}\rightarrow\infty.

∎
